I don’t know if it’s about escape exactly, but… yeah, it feels hard to relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid going to certain places or situations, like the train or meetings, because of how you feel or the anxiety you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do avoid it sometimes. I’ve been trading shifts when I can, or pushing back release windows so I don’t have to be there late.\n\nAnd if I do go in, I’ll take the side entrance. Or I just work from home if I can. Walking through the lobby after dark is… hard. It feels like I’m going to fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you feel that your anxiety in these situations, like on the train or in meetings, is specifically about feeling like you can't escape or might not be able to get help if something happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that clearly. I don’t usually think it through like that.\n\nI just… want to know I can leave if I need to. I don’t know if it’s about getting panicky, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided certain places or situations because you were afraid you might feel extremely anxious or panicky and not be able to get out or reach help if you needed to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I mean… I avoid some things sometimes, but I’m not sure it’s because I think I won’t be able to get help.\n\nIt’s more just… if a place feels too closed in or I don’t know how I’d leave, I’d rather not deal with it. I don’t really think past that.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid places or situations, like the train or meetings, is it because you're afraid you might start feeling extremely anxious or panicky and not be able to leave or get out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it that. I’m not really thinking, like, “I’m going to panic.” It’s more just… if I can’t see an easy way to leave, I get uncomfortable and I’d rather avoid it.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid places or situations like the train or meetings, is it because you're afraid that you might start feeling extremely anxious or panicky and not be able to leave or get out?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure.
